Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today. I really appreciate it, and I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time. 

Um... I'm here because... well, I want to make sure I'm handling things appropriately. I don't want to sound difficult or like a complainer, but for months now I’ve been seeing this same pattern. My sister’s appointments keep getting moved around, bills show up completely wrong, and people just somehow "forget" to call me back. 

Everyone keeps telling me that it’s all just honest mistakes, and I know people get busy—I supervise an office myself, so I always try to be understanding—but... I just know they’re keeping information from me. It really feels like they're trying to push me out of making decisions for her. 

I hope I don't sound unreasonable saying all that? I just try to be so careful and do the right thing, but I know what I'm seeing. Is it okay that I came in to talk to you about this?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I... well, I really hate to use the word 'intentional,' because I know how negative that sounds, and I'd never want to accuse anyone unfairly. I always try to see the best in people. But... yes. I do think so. 

It’s just... it's the constant pattern of it all. Like I mentioned, the appointments constantly getting moved around, the bills being completely wrong, and the way they just happen to 'forget' to call me back. When everyone tells me it’s just honest mistakes, I really try to smile and be polite about it. I don't want to make a fuss. But deep down, I just know they’re keeping information from me. It feels like they're deliberately trying to push me out of making decisions for her care. 

Does that make sense? I hope I don't sound paranoid or difficult saying that to you... I really don't want to be a burden. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing for my sister. Do you think I'm overreacting by seeing it that way?

Do you think I'm overreacting by seeing it that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else is aware of this information being kept from you?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't... I don't really know for sure if anyone else is aware. I mean, I try not to bring it up with other people because I don't want to seem like I'm gossiping or causing trouble. I know everyone at the facility works very hard, and I'd hate to make anyone look bad if I'm... if I'm just misinterpreting things. \n\nI haven't really asked anyone else if they've noticed it. I suppose it's possible that someone else might see what's happening, but... I wouldn't want to put them in an awkward position by asking. I try to keep things pleasant and professional, you know? \n\nDo you think I should be asking other people about it? I just... I don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill if it's just something between me and the people handling her care. I hope it's okay that I haven't really looked into whether anyone else knows.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think this belief that people are keeping information from you is something that might not be based in reality?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 59 / first_line_supervisor_of_office_or_administrative_support_worker / bachelors\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the same pattern—my sister’s appointments get moved, bills show up wrong, people 'forget' to call me back—and even when everyone says it’s just mistakes, I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of decisions.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

Oh... um... well, I really appreciate you asking that, and I know you're just trying to help me look at all the angles. I want to be completely open with you, because I know that's the right thing to do.

I know how that must sound... but I just keep seeing the exact same things over and over. I'm sorry, I hope that doesn't make me sound difficult or... or disconnected from reality. I just want to be a good sister and make sure she's taken care of. Does that make sense? I hope you can understand why I feel so sure about what I'm seeing.

My sister’s appointments keep getting moved around, bills show up completely wrong, and people just somehow \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nEveryone keeps telling me that it’s all just honest mistakes, and I know people get busy—I supervise an office myself, so I always try to be understanding—but... I just know they’re keeping information from me. It really feels like they're trying to push me out of making decisions for her. \n\nI hope I don't sound unreasonable saying all that? I just try to be so careful and do the right thing, but I know what I'm seeing. Is it okay that I came in to talk to you about this?\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are intentionally trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: I... well, I really hate to use the word 'intentional,' because I know how negative that sounds, and I'd never want to accuse anyone unfairly. I always try to see the best in people. But... yes. I do think so. \n\nIt’s just... it's the constant pattern of it all. Like I mentioned, the appointments constantly getting moved around, the bills being completely wrong, and the way they just happen to 'forget' to call me back. When everyone tells me it’s just honest mistakes, I really try to smile and be polite about it. I don't want to make a fuss. But deep down, I just know they’re keeping information from me. It feels like they're deliberately trying to push me out of making decisions for her care. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I don't sound paranoid or difficult saying that to you... I really don't want to be a burden. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing for my sister. Do you think I'm overreacting by seeing it that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else is aware of this information being kept from you?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't... I don't really know for sure if anyone else is aware. I mean, I try not to bring it up with other people because I don't want to seem like I'm gossiping or causing trouble. I know everyone at the facility works very hard, and I'd hate to make anyone look bad if I'm... if I'm just misinterpreting things. \n\nI haven't really asked anyone else if they've noticed it. I suppose it's possible that someone else might see what's happening, but... I wouldn't want to put them in an awkward position by asking. I try to keep things pleasant and professional, you know? \n\nDo you think I should be asking other people about it? I just... I don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill if it's just something between me and the people handling her care. I hope it's okay that I haven't really looked into whether anyone else knows.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this belief that people are keeping information from you is something that might not be based in reality?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, I really appreciate you asking that, and I know you're just trying to help me look at all the angles. I want to be completely open with you, because I know that's the right thing to do. \n\nBut... no, I don't think I'm imagining it. I really don't want to sound stubborn or like I'm not listening to reason. I know how that must sound... but I just keep seeing the exact same things over and over. Like I was saying before, the appointments getting shuffled around, the bills being completely wrong, the unreturned phone calls. \n\nI know everyone says they're just honest mistakes, and I always try to give people the benefit of the doubt—I really do. But it just feels so coordinated. I know in my heart they are keeping things from me and trying to push me out of making decisions for her. \n\nI'm sorry, I hope that doesn't make me sound difficult or... or disconnected from reality. I just want to be a good sister and make sure she's taken care of. Does that make sense? I hope you can understand why I feel so sure about what I'm seeing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that people are keeping information from you about your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... let me think. I want to make sure I give you the exact right timeline, so I can be as helpful as possible. I really hope saying this doesn't make me seem like someone who just... holds onto negativity or looks for problems, because I always try to be a team player and keep things positive. \n\nBut... I'd say it's been going on for about 11 months now. It started right around the time my sister's health suddenly took a turn for the worse, and I had to step in and start coordinating all her care. I just wanted to be a good sister and help organize everything for her, you know? But that's when all these issues started happening. \n\nDoes that timeline help? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly and giving you what you need.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Oh... um, that's a really thoughtful question. I know you're asking because you want to help me see things clearly, and I really, truly appreciate that. I want to be reasonable, I do. I always try to be the kind of person who listens to others and takes feedback well.

Oh, um... well, I really hope this doesn't make me sound like I'm difficult to be around, because I always try so hard to be pleasant and keep the peace with everyone. I really pride myself on being a supportive person.

I just want to make sure things are done correctly. But I have noticed it's starting to affect how people respond to me... they seem a little more guarded or hesitant around me now. 

I'm still fully managing all my responsibilities, though! The office is running smoothly, I promise. I just... I don't want to be the kind of boss or sister who pushes people away. Do you think it's understandable that this kind of stress might spill over just a little bit? I really am trying my best to keep it all together.

I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this belief that people are keeping information from you would change even if you were shown evidence that it's not true?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, that's a really thoughtful question. I know you're asking because you want to help me see things clearly, and I really, truly appreciate that. I want to be reasonable, I do. I always try to be the kind of person who listens to others and takes feedback well. \n\nBut... I just don't know how any evidence could change what I've been living through for months now. I mean, I've seen the same pattern over and over, you know? Like I mentioned before... my sister's appointments getting moved without anyone telling me, the bills showing up completely wrong, people constantly \"forgetting\" to call me back. \n\nI know everyone keeps telling me it's just honest mistakes or administrative errors, and I really wish I could just nod and agree with them so I wouldn't be a bother. But even when they say that, deep down, I just know they're keeping information from me. I know they're trying to push me out of making decisions for her. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that probably makes me sound terribly stubborn, and I really don't want to be difficult. I just... I really don't want you to think I'm just sitting around obsessing over this or letting it ruin my whole life, because I'm not. I try very hard to stay productive and positive, and I keep up with my work at the office and all my other responsibilities. I always try to keep a good balance, you know?\n\nBut... it is on my mind a lot. Especially when I have to deal with the clinic or the insurance company. It just sort of... pops up, because I know I have to be on guard to make sure my sister gets what she needs. I try to push it aside when I'm doing other things, but it's hard not to worry when you feel like you're the only one looking out for someone you love. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound too anxious or fixated on it. I just want to be responsible. Is it normal to worry about things like that when you're taking care of a family member?\n\nDoctor: Is your belief that people are keeping information from you affecting your relationships with friends or family outside of your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really hope this doesn't make me sound like I'm difficult to be around, because I always try so hard to be pleasant and keep the peace with everyone. I really pride myself on being a supportive person. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I do want to give you the full picture... I guess things have gotten just a little bit tense at our family gatherings lately. Nothing awful! Just... a bit strained. \n\nAnd at the office... well, I supervise the administrative team, and I've had a couple of mildly uncomfortable conversations recently. My sister’s appointments keep getting moved around, bills show up completely wrong, and people just somehow \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nEveryone keeps telling me that it’s all just honest mistakes, and I know people get busy—I supervise an office myself, so I always try to be understanding—but... I just know they’re keeping information from me. It really feels like they're trying to push me out of making decisions for her. \n\nI hope I don't sound unreasonable saying all that? I just try to be so careful and do the right thing, but I know what I'm seeing. Is it okay that I came in to talk to you about this?\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are intentionally trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: I... well, I really hate to use the word 'intentional,' because I know how negative that sounds, and I'd never want to accuse anyone unfairly. I always try to see the best in people. But... yes. I do think so. \n\nIt’s just... it's the constant pattern of it all. Like I mentioned, the appointments constantly getting moved around, the bills being completely wrong, and the way they just happen to 'forget' to call me back. When everyone tells me it’s just honest mistakes, I really try to smile and be polite about it. I don't want to make a fuss. But deep down, I just know they’re keeping information from me. It feels like they're deliberately trying to push me out of making decisions for her care. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I don't sound paranoid or difficult saying that to you... I really don't want to be a burden. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing for my sister. Do you think I'm overreacting by seeing it that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else is aware of this information being kept from you?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't... I don't really know for sure if anyone else is aware. I mean, I try not to bring it up with other people because I don't want to seem like I'm gossiping or causing trouble. I know everyone at the facility works very hard, and I'd hate to make anyone look bad if I'm... if I'm just misinterpreting things. \n\nI haven't really asked anyone else if they've noticed it. I suppose it's possible that someone else might see what's happening, but... I wouldn't want to put them in an awkward position by asking. I try to keep things pleasant and professional, you know? \n\nDo you think I should be asking other people about it? I just... I don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill if it's just something between me and the people handling her care. I hope it's okay that I haven't really looked into whether anyone else knows.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this belief that people are keeping information from you is something that might not be based in reality?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, I really appreciate you asking that, and I know you're just trying to help me look at all the angles. I want to be completely open with you, because I know that's the right thing to do. \n\nBut... no, I don't think I'm imagining it. I really don't want to sound stubborn or like I'm not listening to reason. I know how that must sound... but I just keep seeing the exact same things over and over. Like I was saying before, the appointments getting shuffled around, the bills being completely wrong, the unreturned phone calls. \n\nI know everyone says they're just honest mistakes, and I always try to give people the benefit of the doubt—I really do. But it just feels so coordinated. I know in my heart they are keeping things from me and trying to push me out of making decisions for her. \n\nI'm sorry, I hope that doesn't make me sound difficult or... or disconnected from reality. I just want to be a good sister and make sure she's taken care of. Does that make sense? I hope you can understand why I feel so sure about what I'm seeing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that people are keeping information from you about your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... let me think. I want to make sure I give you the exact right timeline, so I can be as helpful as possible. I really hope saying this doesn't make me seem like someone who just... holds onto negativity or looks for problems, because I always try to be a team player and keep things positive. \n\nBut... I'd say it's been going on for about 11 months now. It started right around the time my sister's health suddenly took a turn for the worse, and I had to step in and start coordinating all her care. I just wanted to be a good sister and help organize everything for her, you know? But that's when all these issues started happening. \n\nDoes that timeline help? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this belief that people are keeping information from you would change even if you were shown evidence that it's not true?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, that's a really thoughtful question. I know you're asking because you want to help me see things clearly, and I really, truly appreciate that. I want to be reasonable, I do. I always try to be the kind of person who listens to others and takes feedback well. \n\nBut... I just don't know how any evidence could change what I've been living through for months now. I mean, I've seen the same pattern over and over, you know? Like I mentioned before... my sister's appointments getting moved without anyone telling me, the bills showing up completely wrong, people constantly \"forgetting\" to call me back. \n\nI know everyone keeps telling me it's just honest mistakes or administrative errors, and I really wish I could just nod and agree with them so I wouldn't be a bother. But even when they say that, deep down, I just know they're keeping information from me. I know they're trying to push me out of making decisions for her. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that probably makes me sound terribly stubborn, and I really don't want to be difficult. I just... I can't unsee what's happening. Does that make sense? I hope you're not disappointed in me for feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your belief about people keeping information from you is something that you think about all the time?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, I wouldn't say *all* the time. I really don't want you to think I'm just sitting around obsessing over this or letting it ruin my whole life, because I'm not. I try very hard to stay productive and positive, and I keep up with my work at the office and all my other responsibilities. I always try to keep a good balance, you know?\n\nBut... it is on my mind a lot. Especially when I have to deal with the clinic or the insurance company. It just sort of... pops up, because I know I have to be on guard to make sure my sister gets what she needs. I try to push it aside when I'm doing other things, but it's hard not to worry when you feel like you're the only one looking out for someone you love. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound too anxious or fixated on it. I just want to be responsible. Is it normal to worry about things like that when you're taking care of a family member?\n\nDoctor: Is your belief that people are keeping information from you affecting your relationships with friends or family outside of your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really hope this doesn't make me sound like I'm difficult to be around, because I always try so hard to be pleasant and keep the peace with everyone. I really pride myself on being a supportive person. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I do want to give you the full picture... I guess things have gotten just a little bit tense at our family gatherings lately. Nothing awful! Just... a bit strained. \n\nAnd at the office... well, I supervise the administrative team, and I've had a couple of mildly uncomfortable conversations recently. I think because I'm so on edge about all the \"errors\" with my sister's care, I've caught myself pressing my own staff a bit harder than I used to when they make just, you know, routine mistakes. I just want to make sure things are done correctly. But I have noticed it's starting to affect how people respond to me... they seem a little more guarded or hesitant around me now. \n\nI'm still fully managing all my responsibilities, though! The office is running smoothly, I promise. I just... I don't want to be the kind of boss or sister who pushes people away. Do you think it's understandable that this kind of stress might spill over just a little bit? I really am trying my best to keep it all together.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself questioning the intentions of people in other areas of your life, beyond your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, no, not really. I mean, I really try to see the best in people, I truly do. I always want to assume everyone has good intentions, especially at work or with my friends. I don't want you to think I'm just walking around suspicious of everyone I meet, because that's not me at all. I'm usually very trusting. \n\nIt's really just... just this specific situation with my sister's care, because it's so important to me and I just keep seeing the same things happen over and over with the clinic and the insurance. But outside of that? No, I don't think so. I mean, my staff at the office, like I said, I might be a little more strict lately, but I don't think they're out to get me or anything. I know they're just doing their jobs. \n\nI really try to keep things separate, you know? I hope that makes sense. I wouldn't want to be the kind of person who just distrusts everyone for no reason. That wouldn't be very fair, would it?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

But I have noticed it's starting to affect how people respond to me... they seem a little more guarded or hesitant around me now. \n\nI'm still fully managing all my responsibilities, though! The office is running smoothly, I promise. I just... I don't want to be the kind of boss or sister who pushes people away. Do you think it's understandable that this kind of stress might spill over just a little bit? I really am trying my best to keep it all together.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself questioning the intentions of people in other areas of your life, beyond your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, no, not really. I mean, I really try to see the best in people, I truly do. I always want to assume everyone has good intentions, especially at work or with my friends. I don't want you to think I'm just walking around suspicious of everyone I meet, because that's not me at all. I'm usually very trusting. \n\nIt's really just... just this specific situation with my sister's care, because it's so important to me and I just keep seeing the same things happen over and over with the clinic and the insurance. But outside of that? No, I don't think so. I mean, my staff at the office, like I said, I might be a little more strict lately, but I don't think they're out to get me or anything. I know they're just doing their jobs. \n\nI really try to keep things separate, you know? I hope that makes sense. I wouldn't want to be the kind of person who just distrusts everyone for no reason. That wouldn't be very fair, would it?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often find yourself doubting people's honesty in general, outside of the situation with your sister's care?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 59 / first_line_supervisor_of_office_or_administrative_support_worker / bachelors\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh... um... well, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that I'm just a generally suspicious person, because I'm truly not. I try very hard to be open and trusting with people. 

Like I was saying before, it's really just... just focused on my sister's situation. I don't go around doubting my friends or my neighbors or the people I work with. I mean, I know people are generally good and honest, and I always want to treat them with that same respect. I'd hate to be someone who just assumes the worst of people in my everyday life. 

So, no, I don't doubt people's honesty in general. I try to be very fair-minded about things. I think that's the right way to be, don't you?

Like I was saying before, the appointments getting shuffled around, the bills being completely wrong, the unreturned phone calls. \n\nI know everyone says they're just honest mistakes, and I always try to give people the benefit of the doubt—I really do. But it just feels so coordinated. I know in my heart they are keeping things from me and trying to push me out of making decisions for her. \n\nI'm sorry, I hope that doesn't make me sound difficult or... or disconnected from reality. I just want to be a good sister and make sure she's taken care of. Does that make sense? I hope you can understand why I feel so sure about what I'm seeing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that people are keeping information from you about your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... let me think. I want to make sure I give you the exact right timeline, so I can be as helpful as possible. I really hope saying this doesn't make me seem like someone who just... holds onto negativity or looks for problems, because I always try to be a team player and keep things positive. \n\nBut... I'd say it's been going on for about 11 months now. It started right around the time my sister's health suddenly took a turn for the worse, and I had to step in and start coordinating all her care. I just wanted to be a good sister and help organize everything for her, you know? But that's when all these issues started happening. \n\nDoes that timeline help? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this belief that people are keeping information from you would change even if you were shown evidence that it's not true?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, that's a really thoughtful question. I know you're asking because you want to help me see things clearly, and I really, truly appreciate that. I want to be reasonable, I do. I always try to be the kind of person who listens to others and takes feedback well. \n\nBut... I just don't know how any evidence could change what I've been living through for months now. I mean, I've seen the same pattern over and over, you know? Like I mentioned before... my sister's appointments getting moved without anyone telling me, the bills showing up completely wrong, people constantly \"forgetting\" to call me back. \n\nI know everyone keeps telling me it's just honest mistakes or administrative errors, and I really wish I could just nod and agree with them so I wouldn't be a bother. But even when they say that, deep down, I just know they're keeping information from me. I know they're trying to push me out of making decisions for her. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that probably makes me sound terribly stubborn, and I really don't want to be difficult. I just... I can't unsee what's happening. Does that make sense? I hope you're not disappointed in me for feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your belief about people keeping information from you is something that you think about all the time?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, I wouldn't say *all* the time. I really don't want you to think I'm just sitting around obsessing over this or letting it ruin my whole life, because I'm not. I try very hard to stay productive and positive, and I keep up with my work at the office and all my other responsibilities. I always try to keep a good balance, you know?\n\nBut... it is on my mind a lot. Especially when I have to deal with the clinic or the insurance company. It just sort of... pops up, because I know I have to be on guard to make sure my sister gets what she needs. I try to push it aside when I'm doing other things, but it's hard not to worry when you feel like you're the only one looking out for someone you love. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound too anxious or fixated on it. I just want to be responsible. Is it normal to worry about things like that when you're taking care of a family member?\n\nDoctor: Is your belief that people are keeping information from you affecting your relationships with friends or family outside of your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really hope this doesn't make me sound like I'm difficult to be around, because I always try so hard to be pleasant and keep the peace with everyone. I really pride myself on being a supportive person. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I do want to give you the full picture... I guess things have gotten just a little bit tense at our family gatherings lately. Nothing awful! Just... a bit strained. \n\nAnd at the office... well, I supervise the administrative team, and I've had a couple of mildly uncomfortable conversations recently. I think because I'm so on edge about all the \"errors\" with my sister's care, I've caught myself pressing my own staff a bit harder than I used to when they make just, you know, routine mistakes. I just want to make sure things are done correctly. But I have noticed it's starting to affect how people respond to me... they seem a little more guarded or hesitant around me now. \n\nI'm still fully managing all my responsibilities, though! The office is running smoothly, I promise. I just... I don't want to be the kind of boss or sister who pushes people away. Do you think it's understandable that this kind of stress might spill over just a little bit? I really am trying my best to keep it all together.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself questioning the intentions of people in other areas of your life, beyond your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, no, not really. I don't want you to think I'm just walking around suspicious of everyone I meet, because that's not me at all. I'm usually very trusting. \n\nIt's really just... just this specific situation with my sister's care, because it's so important to me and I just keep seeing the same things happen over and over with the clinic and the insurance. But outside of that? No, I don't think so. I mean, my staff at the office, like I said, I might be a little more strict lately, but I don't think they're out to get me or anything. I know they're just doing their jobs. \n\nI really try to keep things separate, you know? I hope that makes sense. I wouldn't want to be the kind of person who just distrusts everyone for no reason. That wouldn't be very fair, would it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doubting people's honesty in general, outside of the situation with your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that I'm just a generally suspicious person, because I'm truly not. I try very hard to be open and trusting with people. \n\nLike I was saying before, it's really just... just focused on my sister's situation. I don't go around doubting my friends or my neighbors or the people I work with. I mean, I know people are generally good and honest, and I always want to treat them with that same respect. I'd hate to be someone who just assumes the worst of people in my everyday life. \n\nSo, no, I don't doubt people's honesty in general. I try to be very fair-minded about things. I think that's the right way to be, don't you?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself having thoughts that might seem strange or unrealistic to others in your daily life, outside of the issues with your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really hope I haven't sounded like I'm... well, like I'm losing my grip on things or acting bizarrely. I try so hard to be a grounded, sensible person. \n\nHonestly, outside of dealing with my sister's appointments and just... trying so hard to get straight answers from those people, everything else is completely normal. I still keep my home perfectly in order, I pay all my bills on time, and I'm doing my job well, like I mentioned. \n\nI know some people might think I'm a little too focused on the clinic situation, and maybe I am, but I'm certainly not falling apart or acting strangely in other parts of my life. I'm just a responsible sister trying to handle a difficult situation. That doesn't make me sound unreasonable, does it? I really am holding everything else together just fine.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Oh, goodness, no. No, I really don't. 

Um... I hope I haven't made you think my mind is playing tricks on me or anything. I promise I don't have anything like that happening. That sounds like it would be quite frightening, actually, but thankfully I've never dealt with that. 

I'm a very practical person, you know? You really have to be grounded to supervise an office and keep everything running smoothly. So, no, I don't get stuck on thoughts that aren't real. My mind is very clear. 

I'm making sense, aren't I? I just really want to assure you that outside of making sure my sister is safe and getting the right care, I'm completely level-headed. I wouldn't want you to think I was imagining things.

Interfered? Oh, no, no. I am still managing all my responsibilities perfectly well, I assure you. I would never let my work slip. 

I mean... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, maybe it has bled over just a tiny bit? But not in a way that stops me from doing my job! I really don't want to be a difficult person to work with, or to be around. It's understandable to be a little on edge and expect more from people when you're dealing with a stressful family situation, isn't it? I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad supervisor. I really am trying my best to keep everything pleasant.

When everyone tells me it’s just honest mistakes, I really try to smile and be polite about it. I don't want to make a fuss. But deep down, I just know they’re keeping information from me. It feels like they're deliberately trying to push me out of making decisions for her care. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I don't sound paranoid or difficult saying that to you... I really don't want to be a burden. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing for my sister. Do you think I'm overreacting by seeing it that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else is aware of this information being kept from you?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't... I don't really know for sure if anyone else is aware. I mean, I try not to bring it up with other people because I don't want to seem like I'm gossiping or causing trouble. I know everyone at the facility works very hard, and I'd hate to make anyone look bad if I'm... if I'm just misinterpreting things. \n\nI haven't really asked anyone else if they've noticed it. I suppose it's possible that someone else might see what's happening, but... I wouldn't want to put them in an awkward position by asking. I try to keep things pleasant and professional, you know? \n\nDo you think I should be asking other people about it? I just... I don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill if it's just something between me and the people handling her care. I hope it's okay that I haven't really looked into whether anyone else knows.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this belief that people are keeping information from you is something that might not be based in reality?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, I really appreciate you asking that, and I know you're just trying to help me look at all the angles. I want to be completely open with you, because I know that's the right thing to do. \n\nBut... no, I don't think I'm imagining it. I really don't want to sound stubborn or like I'm not listening to reason. I know how that must sound... but I just keep seeing the exact same things over and over. Like I was saying before, the appointments getting shuffled around, the bills being completely wrong, the unreturned phone calls. \n\nI know everyone says they're just honest mistakes, and I always try to give people the benefit of the doubt—I really do. But it just feels so coordinated. I know in my heart they are keeping things from me and trying to push me out of making decisions for her. \n\nI'm sorry, I hope that doesn't make me sound difficult or... or disconnected from reality. I just want to be a good sister and make sure she's taken care of. Does that make sense? I hope you can understand why I feel so sure about what I'm seeing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that people are keeping information from you about your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... let me think. I want to make sure I give you the exact right timeline, so I can be as helpful as possible. I really hope saying this doesn't make me seem like someone who just... holds onto negativity or looks for problems, because I always try to be a team player and keep things positive. \n\nBut... I'd say it's been going on for about 11 months now. It started right around the time my sister's health suddenly took a turn for the worse, and I had to step in and start coordinating all her care. I just wanted to be a good sister and help organize everything for her, you know? But that's when all these issues started happening. \n\nDoes that timeline help? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this belief that people are keeping information from you would change even if you were shown evidence that it's not true?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, that's a really thoughtful question. I know you're asking because you want to help me see things clearly, and I really, truly appreciate that. I want to be reasonable, I do. I always try to be the kind of person who listens to others and takes feedback well. \n\nBut... I just don't know how any evidence could change what I've been living through for months now. Like I mentioned before... my sister's appointments getting moved without anyone telling me, the bills showing up completely wrong, people constantly \"forgetting\" to call me back. \n\nI know everyone keeps telling me it's just honest mistakes or administrative errors, and I really wish I could just nod and agree with them so I wouldn't be a bother. But even when they say that, deep down, I just know they're keeping information from me. I know they're trying to push me out of making decisions for her. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that probably makes me sound terribly stubborn, and I really don't want to be difficult. I just... I can't unsee what's happening. Does that make sense? I really don't want you to think I'm just sitting around obsessing over this or letting it ruin my whole life, because I'm not. I try very hard to stay productive and positive, and I keep up with my work at the office and all my other responsibilities. I always try to keep a good balance, you know?\n\nBut... it is on my mind a lot. Especially when I have to deal with the clinic or the insurance company. It just sort of... pops up, because I know I have to be on guard to make sure my sister gets what she needs. I try to push it aside when I'm doing other things, but it's hard not to worry when you feel like you're the only one looking out for someone you love. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound too anxious or fixated on it. I just want to be responsible. Is it normal to worry about things like that when you're taking care of a family member?\n\nDoctor: Is your belief that people are keeping information from you affecting your relationships with friends or family outside of your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really hope this doesn't make me sound like I'm difficult to be around, because I always try so hard to be pleasant and keep the peace with everyone. I really pride myself on being a supportive person. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I do want to give you the full picture... Do you think it's understandable that this kind of stress might spill over just a little bit? I really am trying my best to keep it all together.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself questioning the intentions of people in other areas of your life, beyond your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, no, not really. I mean, I really try to see the best in people, I truly do. I always want to assume everyone has good intentions, especially at work or with my friends. I don't want you to think I'm just walking around suspicious of everyone I meet, because that's not me at all. I'm usually very trusting. \n\nIt's really just... just this specific situation with my sister's care, because it's so important to me and I just keep seeing the same things happen over and over with the clinic and the insurance. But outside of that? No, I don't think so. I mean, my staff at the office, like I said, I might be a little more strict lately, but I don't think they're out to get me or anything. I know they're just doing their jobs. \n\nI really try to keep things separate, you know? I hope that makes sense. I wouldn't want to be the kind of person who just distrusts everyone for no reason. That wouldn't be very fair, would it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doubting people's honesty in general, outside of the situation with your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, no, I wouldn't say that at all. Do you think I'm overreacting by seeing it that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else is aware of this information being kept from you?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't... I don't really know for sure if anyone else is aware. I mean, I try not to bring it up with other people because I don't want to seem like I'm gossiping or causing trouble. I know everyone at the facility works very hard, and I'd hate to make anyone look bad if I'm... if I'm just misinterpreting things. \n\nI haven't really asked anyone else if they've noticed it. I suppose it's possible that someone else might see what's happening, but... I wouldn't want to put them in an awkward position by asking. I try to keep things pleasant and professional, you know? \n\nDo you think I should be asking other people about it? I just... I don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill if it's just something between me and the people handling her care. I hope it's okay that I haven't really looked into whether anyone else knows.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this belief that people are keeping information from you is something that might not be based in reality?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, I really appreciate you asking that, and I know you're just trying to help me look at all the angles. I want to be completely open with you, because I know that's the right thing to do. \n\nBut... no, I don't think I'm imagining it. I really don't want to sound stubborn or like I'm not listening to reason. I know how that must sound... but I just keep seeing the exact same things over and over. Like I was saying before, the appointments getting shuffled around, the bills being completely wrong, the unreturned phone calls. \n\nI know everyone says they're just honest mistakes, and I always try to give people the benefit of the doubt—I really do. But it just feels so coordinated. I know in my heart they are keeping things from me and trying to push me out of making decisions for her. \n\nI'm sorry, I hope that doesn't make me sound difficult or... or disconnected from reality. I just want to be a good sister and make sure she's taken care of. Does that make sense? I hope you can understand why I feel so sure about what I'm seeing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that people are keeping information from you about your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... let me think. I want to make sure I give you the exact right timeline, so I can be as helpful as possible. I really hope saying this doesn't make me seem like someone who just... holds onto negativity or looks for problems, because I always try to be a team player and keep things positive. \n\nBut... I'd say it's been going on for about 11 months now. It started right around the time my sister's health suddenly took a turn for the worse, and I had to step in and start coordinating all her care. I just wanted to be a good sister and help organize everything for her, you know? But that's when all these issues started happening. \n\nDoes that timeline help? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this belief that people are keeping information from you would change even if you were shown evidence that it's not true?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, that's a really thoughtful question. I know you're asking because you want to help me see things clearly, and I really, truly appreciate that. I want to be reasonable, I do. I always try to be the kind of person who listens to others and takes feedback well. \n\nBut... I just don't know how any evidence could change what I've been living through for months now. I mean, I've seen the same pattern over and over, you know? Like I mentioned before... my sister's appointments getting moved without anyone telling me, the bills showing up completely wrong, people constantly \"forgetting\" to call me back. \n\nI know everyone keeps telling me it's just honest mistakes or administrative errors, and I really wish I could just nod and agree with them so I wouldn't be a bother. But even when they say that, deep down, I just know they're keeping information from me. I know they're trying to push me out of making decisions for her. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that probably makes me sound terribly stubborn, and I really don't want to be difficult. I just... I can't unsee what's happening. Does that make sense? I hope you're not disappointed in me for feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your belief about people keeping information from you is something that you think about all the time?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, I wouldn't say *all* the time. I really don't want you to think I'm just sitting around obsessing over this or letting it ruin my whole life, because I'm not. I try very hard to stay productive and positive, and I keep up with my work at the office and all my other responsibilities. I always try to keep a good balance, you know?\n\nBut... it is on my mind a lot. Especially when I have to deal with the clinic or the insurance company. It just sort of... pops up, because I know I have to be on guard to make sure my sister gets what she needs. I try to push it aside when I'm doing other things, but it's hard not to worry when you feel like you're the only one looking out for someone you love. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound too anxious or fixated on it. I just want to be responsible. Is it normal to worry about things like that when you're taking care of a family member?\n\nDoctor: Is your belief that people are keeping information from you affecting your relationships with friends or family outside of your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really hope this doesn't make me sound like I'm difficult to be around, because I always try so hard to be pleasant and keep the peace with everyone. I really pride myself on being a supportive person. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I do want to give you the full picture... I guess things have gotten just a little bit tense at our family gatherings lately. Nothing awful! Just... a bit strained. \n\nAnd at the office... well, I supervise the administrative team, and I've had a couple of mildly uncomfortable conversations recently. I think because I'm so on edge about all the \"errors\" with my sister's care, I've caught myself pressing my own staff a bit harder than I used to when they make just, you know, routine mistakes. I just want to make sure things are done correctly. But I have noticed it's starting to affect how people respond to me... they seem a little more guarded or hesitant around me now. \n\nI'm still fully managing all my responsibilities, though! The office is running smoothly, I promise. I just... I don't want to be the kind of boss or sister who pushes people away. Do you think it's understandable that this kind of stress might spill over just a little bit? I really am trying my best to keep it all together.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself questioning the intentions of people in other areas of your life, beyond your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, no, not really. I mean, I really try to see the best in people, I truly do. I always want to assume everyone has good intentions, especially at work or with my friends. I don't want you to think I'm just walking around suspicious of everyone I meet, because that's not me at all. I'm usually very trusting. \n\nIt's really just... just this specific situation with my sister's care, because it's so important to me and I just keep seeing the same things happen over and over with the clinic and the insurance. But outside of that? No, I don't think so. I mean, my staff at the office, like I said, I might be a little more strict lately, but I don't think they're out to get me or anything. I know they're just doing their jobs. \n\nI really try to keep things separate, you know? I hope that makes sense. I wouldn't want to be the kind of person who just distrusts everyone for no reason. That wouldn't be very fair, would it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doubting people's honesty in general, outside of the situation with your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that I'm just a generally suspicious person, because I'm truly not. I try very hard to be open and trusting with people. \n\nLike I was saying before, it's really just... just focused on my sister's situation. I don't go around doubting my friends or my neighbors or the people I work with. I mean, I know people are generally good and honest, and I always want to treat them with that same respect. I'd hate to be someone who just assumes the worst of people in my everyday life. \n\nSo, no, I don't doubt people's honesty in general. I try to be very fair-minded about things. I think that's the right way to be, don't you?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself having thoughts that might seem strange or unrealistic to others in your daily life, outside of the issues with your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really hope I haven't sounded like I'm... well, like I'm losing my grip on things or acting bizarrely. I try so hard to be a grounded, sensible person. \n\nHonestly, outside of dealing with my sister's appointments and just... trying so hard to get straight answers from those people, everything else is completely normal. I still keep my home perfectly in order, I pay all my bills on time, and I'm doing my job well, like I mentioned. \n\nI know some people might think I'm a little too focused on the clinic situation, and maybe I am, but I'm certainly not falling apart or acting strangely in other parts of my life. I'm just a responsible sister trying to handle a difficult situation. That doesn't make me sound unreasonable, does it? I really am holding everything else together just fine.\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety does this belief cause you on a daily basis?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, I really wouldn't call it distress. I hope I haven't made it sound like I'm a nervous wreck or anything, because I'm truly not! \n\nI mean, naturally, it's a little stressful sometimes, just wanting to make sure she gets the proper care and having to stay on top of the clinic. But I manage it very well. I'm quite organized, you know, being a supervisor and all, so I don't let it overwhelm me. I don't sit around wringing my hands or feeling anxious all day. I just... I just do what needs to be done. \n\nI think anyone looking out for their family would feel a tiny bit of pressure to get things right, don't you think? But no, no real anxiety. I try to stay very positive and keep a good handle on my emotions. I think I'm doing a pretty good job of balancing it all, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that you knew were not true but couldn't shake, outside of the issues with your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I really don't. \n\nUm... I hope I haven't made you think my mind is playing tricks on me or anything. I promise I don't have anything like that happening. That sounds like it would be quite frightening, actually, but thankfully I've never dealt with that. \n\nI'm a very practical person, you know? You really have to be grounded to supervise an office and keep everything running smoothly. So, no, I don't get stuck on thoughts that aren't real. My mind is very clear. \n\nI'm making sense, aren't I? I just really want to assure you that outside of making sure my sister is safe and getting the right care, I'm completely level-headed. I wouldn't want you to think I was imagining things.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that people are keeping information from you about your sister's care interfered with your ability to perform your job or daily activities?\n\nPatient: Interfered? Oh, no, no. I am still managing all my responsibilities perfectly well, I assure you. I would never let my work slip. \n\nI mean... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, maybe it has bled over just a tiny bit? But not in a way that stops me from doing my job! It's just... well, our family meetings have gotten a little tense lately. And at the office, I've had a few... slightly uncomfortable conversations. I think I've just been pressing my staff a little harder about routine mistakes than I used to. You know, just expecting things to be done correctly, especially since I have to be so vigilant with the clinic. \n\nI'm still getting all my work done, of course. But I have noticed that... well, it has started to affect how people respond to me, just a little. They seem a bit more guarded around me now. I really don't want to be a difficult person to work with, or to be around. It's understandable to be a little on edge and expect more from people when you're dealing with a stressful family situation, isn't it? I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad supervisor. I really am trying my best to keep everything pleasant.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that you might be misinterpreting the actions of the people involved in your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, I really do try to keep an open mind. I don't want to be one of those difficult people who can't admit when they might be wrong, you know? I always try to look at things objectively. But... um... in this case, I really don't think I'm misinterpreting things. I hope that doesn't make me sound unreasonable to you. \n\nIt's just that... well, I’m here because for months now, I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern over and over. My sister’s appointments will suddenly get moved without any notice, her bills show up wrong, and the staff constantly just 'forget' to call me back. And I know everyone there says it’s just innocent clerical mistakes, and I really, really try to give people the benefit of the doubt! But... I just know they’re keeping information from me. It's so clear that they're trying to push me out of the decisions about her care. \n\nAny good supervisor would notice a pattern like that when it happens this often, don't you agree? I just want to do right by my sister, I promise I'm not just trying to be stubborn.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, dear... I'm so sorry, did I completely misunderstand you a moment ago? I know we just touched on this, and I really don't want to frustrate you by making you ask the same sort of thing twice. Am I not explaining myself very well?

Oh... an illusion? Um... I can completely understand why you might ask that, and I really, really don't want to sound unreasonable or difficult to you. I'd hate for you to think I'm just imagining things or being a nuisance. 

But... well, I've been seeing the exact same pattern for months now, so it's very real to me. It's actually why I'm here. My sister's appointments keep getting moved around without anyone telling me, her bills show up wrong, and the staff constantly just "forget" to call me back. I know everyone there tells me they're just innocent administrative mistakes, and I try so hard to be understanding—I supervise an office myself, so I know slip-ups happen! 

But... I just know they're keeping information from me. It's too consistent. It really feels like they are actively trying to push me out of the decisions regarding her care. 

I hope you don't think badly of me for believing that. I'm not trying to cause trouble, I just want to protect her. Does that make sense, doctor? I hope I'm explaining my side of things clearly enough for you.

I promise I'm not trying to be difficult or hide anything from you. \n\nI really, truly haven't had anything like that happen. No thoughts getting stuck, nothing that isn't true... just my very real worries about what's happening at the clinic with my sister. Everything else is just... normal everyday things. \n\nI'm so sorry if I'm not giving you the answer you need. Am I missing something? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, dear... I'm so sorry, I feel like I'm repeating myself and I really don't want to bore you or make you think I'm just being stubborn. I know I must sound a bit... intense about all this. I hope you don't think I'm just a hysterical older sister who relies on feelings instead of logic. I really pride myself on being level-headed.

But to answer your question... no, it's definitely not just a feeling. It's based on very real things I've been seeing for months. It's the facts of it—the appointments being moved without telling me, the incorrect bills, the constant "forgetting" to return my calls. 

I know they try to smooth it over and say it's just administrative errors, and I really do try to give them the benefit of the doubt... but when you look at all those facts together, it's just too much of a coincidence. I know they are keeping things from me. I know they're trying to push me out of her care. 

I'm so sorry, I don't mean to sound argumentative. I just... I see the pattern, and I have to act on it to protect her. Does that seem reasonable to you, doctor? I really hope you can see where I'm coming from.
